[SYSTEM]
You are an RTL (Register Transfer Level) design agent. Your task is to create a Verilog/SystemVerilog design that satisfies the given specification, is functionally correct, and has minimal cost (area_delay_product).

**Cost metric `area_delay_product`:** area × achieved critical-path delay — the classic area-delay product. Does NOT include simulation cycle count (contrast with the 'area_runtime_product' metric). Cost is measured on your design alone. The testbench and everything it owns (checking logic, the external memories, etc.) is not synthesized and contributes nothing to area, delay, power, or energy.

## Specification
Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU),
module name `tpu`, implementing the EXACT per-cycle behavior of the logikbench
`tpu` benchmark (zeroasiccorp/logikbench, MIT).

FUNCTION: computes C = A * B tile products on an 8x8 grid of processing
elements. B (the "weight" matrix, 8-bit signed) is loaded into the array and
held; A (the "activation" matrix, 8-bit signed) streams through; result rows
C (32-bit signed) drain out the bottom.

PROTOCOL:
  1. Weight load: assert w_valid for 8 cycles; w_data carries one weight row
     per cycle, 8 signed bytes packed { col 7 ... col 0 }, BOTTOM matrix row
     first (cycle k carries B[7-k][*]; the weights shift south). After 8
     cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
  2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle (packed the
     same way). Row m of the result, C[m][*] packed { col 7 ... col 0 } as 8
     signed 32-bit words, appears on c_data with c_valid high exactly 15
     (= 2*8-1) compute cycles after its A row was accepted.
  - The array advances only while w_valid is low: asserting w_valid stalls
    any in-flight computation (and reloads weights). Zeros are injected while
    a_valid is low, so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
  - Multiple A tiles may stream back-to-back against the same loaded weights.

CYCLE ACCURACY: c_valid is compared against the original design EVERY cycle;
c_data is compared on every cycle where c_valid is high. The 15-cycle
compute-latency and the stall semantics are part of the contract.

Ports:
  clk, rst (async active-high reset, asserted only before the stream begins)
  w_valid, w_data[63:0]
  a_valid, a_data[63:0]
  c_valid, c_data[255:0]

A working starting point is provided: `starting_point.v` is the
original logikbench RTL (tpu_pe / tpu_array / tpu: PE mesh, per-row input
skew, per-column output de-skew, valid pipeline). It passes every cycle of the golden
trace in `vectors.dat` (directed zero/identity/extreme tiles, random tiles,
back-to-back streams, a_valid gaps, mid-drain weight reloads, random soak).
Study it, then optimize while preserving the cycle-accurate behavior. The
sibling benchmark `tpu_spire` shares the identical testbench and vectors
with a SpireHDL starting point.

## Tools
- create_file: Create a new file in your working directory
- replace_file: Replace the entire contents of an existing file
- apply_diff: Apply a unified diff to modify a file
- edit_file: Edit a file by replacing an exact string match with new content. Provide old_string (must match exactly once) and new_string.
- ls: List files in your working directory
- read_file: Read the contents of a file
- run_evaluation(filename[, target_delay]): Run evaluation on the given design file. Returns correctness (Verilator simulation) + cost (Yosys area_delay_product). The filename must be your main design file (e.g. 'design.sv'). Optionally pass target_delay (ps) to override the synthesis timing constraint.

## Strategy
1. Lay out an action plan. Try to cover a diverse set of approaches in your plan to increase the chances of finding a good solution within the step limit. Also once you find a new best solution, explore close solutions. Trade off exploration with exploitation.
2. First, create a simple, straightforward design that is functionally correct. Focus on getting 100% correctness first.
3. Run evaluation to confirm correctness and get the initial cost (area_delay_product).
4. ONLY after achieving 100% correctness, try to optimize the design to reduce the area_delay_product.
5. After each optimization, run evaluation to verify correctness is maintained and check the new cost.
6. If an optimization breaks correctness, revert and try a different approach.
7. Keep iterating until you run out of steps — use every step to try improvements. The run ends automatically at the step limit; your best passing design is kept.

In general, be creative and take risk. At least after you have a correct design, try bold optimizations to reduce area_delay_product, even if they might break correctness. You can always revert if it doesn't work.
The best results you obtain which is 100% correctness counts.
Try to beat at least the straightforward implementation.

Running evaluation is crucial after every change to ensure correctness and track cost. Do not skip evaluations.

You may do some 'thinking' alongside with your tool calling responses to explain your reasoning, but always remember to call a tool in every response.

## Important
- Always call a tool in every response.
- You have a maximum of **60 steps** (LLM turns). Budget them wisely: get a correct design first, then optimise. You may batch tool calls in one response --- at most 8, and only one `run_evaluation`.
- The best result is the one with lowest cost among designs that pass 100% of testbench checks.
- Use different filenames for different design variants so you can retrieve them later.
- If context files (e.g. component source files) are in your workspace, you can modify them directly using edit_file or replace_file to optimize the design. Make targeted, incremental changes rather than rewriting files from scratch — small edits are less likely to introduce bugs.
- Write clean, synthesizable RTL code.
- You can pass `target_delay` (ps) to `run_evaluation` to try different synthesis timing constraints; experimenting with this may improve PPA results (lower target delay may reduce delay, higher target delay may reduce area/transistor count). target_delay must be > 0.
- Do not spend too many steps sweeping `target_delay` — basically if you want to minimize delay choose a low value,if you want to minimize area/transistor count choose a high value. That's it.A few targeted experiments are more productive than an exhaustive sweep.

## Delay Optimization Tips
- `target_delay` trades area against delay (a lower value pressures toward faster logic, a higher value lets Yosys re-map to smaller cells). Do not overly iterate on different target delays; often the RTL structure is the real lever, target_delay is only a minor secondary knob.
- After getting a correct design, try different accumulation orderings (sequential vs. tree) and compare results — Yosys synthesis can produce noticeably different delays (~10 ps) from structurally similar descriptions.
- For designs dominated by a multiply-accumulate chain, the critical path is the multiplier followed by the adder tree. Reducing the adder depth or splitting into parallel partial sums can help.

## Reference Implementations
The following are reference implementations for inspiration. Study them for patterns and techniques, but do not copy them directly — adapt ideas to your specific design.

### Parallel-prefix adder (ripple-carry / carry-lookahead) (Add.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Binary adder using ripple-carry or parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.
// S = A+B

module Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);

	// Function: Binary adder using parallel-prefix carry-lookahead logic.

	logic [width-1:0] GI, PI;  // prefix gen./prop. in
	logic [width-1:0] GO, PO;  // prefix gen./prop. out
	logic [width-1:0] PT;  // adder propagate temp

	// Internal signals for unsigned operands
	logic [width-1:0] Auns, Buns, Suns;

	// default ripple-carry adder as slow implementation
	if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin
		// type conversion: std_logic_vector -> unsigned
		assign Auns = A;
		assign Buns = B;

		// addition
		assign Suns = Auns + Buns;

		// type conversion: unsigned -> std_logic_vector
		assign S = Suns;
	end else begin
		// parallel-prefix adders as medium and fast implementations

		// calculate prefix input generate/propagate signals
		assign GI = A & B;
		assign PI = A | B;
		// calculate adder propagate signals (PT = A xor B)
		assign PT = ~GI & PI;

		// calculate prefix output generate/propagate signals
		PrefixAndOr #(
			.width(width),
			.speed(speed)
		) prefix (
			.GI(GI),
			.PI(PI),
			.GO(GO),
			.PO(PO)
		);

		// calculate sum bits
		assign S = PT ^ {GO[width-2:0], 1'b0};
	end
endmodule



module behavioural_Add #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] A,  // operands
	input  logic [width-1:0] B,
	output logic [width-1:0] S   // sum
);
	assign S = A + B;
endmodule
```

### Prefix AND-OR structures (Sklansky, Brent-Kung, serial) (PrefixAndOr.sv)
```systemverilog
// Copyright 2024 ETH Zurich and University of Bologna.
// Solderpad Hardware License, Version 0.51, see LICENSE for details.
// SPDX-License-Identifier: SHL-0.51
//
// Based on the work by Reto Zimmermann 1998 - ETH Zürich
// Originally written in VHDL, available under: 
// https://iis-people.ee.ethz.ch/~zimmi/arith_lib.html#library
//
// Authors:
// - Thomas Benz <tbenz@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Philippe Sauter <phsauter@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
// - Paul Scheffler <paulsc@iis.ee.ethz.ch>
//
// Description :
// Prefix structures of different depth (i.e. speed) for carry calculation
// in binary adders. Compute in m levels new generate/propagate signal pairs
// for always larger groups of bits. Generate signals of the last level correspond
// to carries in binary addition. Basic logic operations: AND-OR for generate
// signals, AND for propagate signals.

module PrefixAndOr #(
	parameter int              width = 8,             // word width
	parameter lau_pkg::speed_e speed = lau_pkg::FAST  // performance parameter
) (
	input  logic [width-1:0] GI,  // gen./prop. in
	input  logic [width-1:0] PI,  // gen./prop. in
	output logic [width-1:0] GO,  // gen./prop. out
	output logic [width-1:0] PO   // gen./prop. out
);

	// Constants
	localparam int n = width;  // prefix structure width
	localparam int m = $clog2(width);  // prefix structure depth

	// Sklansky parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::FAST) begin : fastPrefix
		logic [(m+1)*n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
			assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
			assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;
			for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels
				for (genvar k = 0; k < 2 ** (m - l); k++) begin : groups
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2 ** (l - 1); i++) begin : bits
						// pass prop and gen to following nodes
						if ((k * 2 ** l + i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = GT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
							assign PT[l*n+k*2**l+i] = PT[(l-1)*n+k*2**l+i];
						end
						// calculate new propagate and generate
						if ((k * 2 ** l + 2 ** (l - 1) + i) < n) begin : black
							assign GT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
												GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
											  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												 & GT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1] );
							assign PT[l*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i] = 
													PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) + i]
												  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) - 1];
						end
					end
				end
			end
			assign GO = GT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
			assign PO = PT[(m+1)*n-1 : m*n];
	end

	// Brent-Kung parallel-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	if (speed == lau_pkg::MEDIUM) begin : mediumPrefix
		logic [(2*m)*n -1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[n-1:0] = GI;
		assign PT[n-1:0] = PI;

		for (genvar l = 1; l <= m; l++) begin : levels1
			for (genvar k = 0; k < 2**(m-l); k++) begin : groups
				for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**l -1; i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**l +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l +i];
					end // white
				end // bits
				if ((k* 2**l + 2**l -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l - 1] |
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							     & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**l + 2**(l-1) -1]);
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**l + 2**l -1] =
								PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**l     -1] 
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k*2**l + 2**(l-1) -1];
				end // black
			end
		end // level1
		for (genvar l = m +1; l < 2*m; l++) begin : levels2
			for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
				if (i < n) begin : white
					assign GT[l*n +i] = GT[(l-1)*n +i];
					assign PT[l*n +i] = PT[(l-1)*n +i];
				end // white
			end // bits
			for (genvar k = 1; k < 2**(l-m); k++) begin : groups
				if (l < 2*m -1) begin : empty
					for (genvar i = 0; i < 2**(2*m -l -1) -1; i++) begin : bits
						if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
							assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
							assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						end // white
					end
				end // empty
				if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l -1) -1) < n) begin : black
					assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  | (  PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) + 2**(2*m-l-1) -1] 
								 & GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m-l) -1] );
					assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1] = 
								PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) + 2**(2*m -l-1) -1]
							  & PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) -1];
				end // black
				for (genvar i = 2**(2*m -l-1); i < 2**(2*m -l); i++) begin : bits
					if ((k* 2**(2*m -l) +i) < n) begin : white
						assign GT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = GT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
						assign PT[l*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i] = PT[(l-1)*n + k* 2**(2*m -l) +i];
					end // white
				end
			end
		end // level2
		assign GO = GT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
		assign PO = PT[2*m*n -1 : (2*m -1) * n];
	end  // Serial-prefix carry-lookahead structure
	else if (speed == lau_pkg::SLOW) begin : slowPrefix
		logic [n-1:0] GT, PT;  // gen./prop. temp
		assign GT[0] = GI[0];
		assign PT[0] = PI[0];
		
		for (genvar i = 1; i < n; i++) begin : bits
			assign GT[i] = GI[i] | (PI[i] & GT[i-1]);
			assign PT[i] = PI[i] & PT[i-1];
		end
		assign GO = GT;
		assign PO = PT;
	end

endmodule

```

[Step 1/60]
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Processing element (PE) for the weight-stationary systolic array (TPU MXU).
//
// Each PE holds one stationary signed weight 'w'. Two independent datapaths
// share the cell:
//
//   Weight load (ld): the weights form a per-column shift register that loads
//   from the top. On each ld cycle the PE captures the weight from the PE above
//   (w_north) and presents its stored weight downward (w_south). After N load
//   cycles every PE in a column holds its final stationary weight.
//
//   Compute (en): the activation flows west to east (registered, one hop per
//   cycle) and the partial sum flows north to south, accumulating the local
//   product a_west * w. This is the only arithmetic cell in the array: one
//   signed DW x DW multiply plus an ACCW-bit add.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_pe
  #(parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator / partial-sum width (signed)
   (
    input		  clk,
    input		  rst,	      // synchronous, active high
    input		  ld,	      // weight-load shift enable
    input		  en,	      // compute enable
    input [DW-1:0]	  w_north,    // weight in from PE above
    input [DW-1:0]	  a_west,     // activation in from west
    input [ACCW-1:0]	  psum_north, // partial sum in from above
    output [DW-1:0]	  w_south,    // stored weight out to PE below
    output reg [DW-1:0]	  a_east,     // activation out to east
    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south  // partial sum out to below
    );

   // stationary weight register; presented downward for the load shift chain
   reg [DW-1:0] w;
   assign w_south = w;

   // local product: the ONLY signed operation. Operands are interpreted as
   // signed here via $signed; the 2*DW-bit result holds the two's-complement
   // product, which is then sign-extended to the accumulator width below.
   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);

   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst) begin
         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};
         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};
         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
      end
      else begin
         if (ld)
           w <= w_north;
         if (en) begin
            a_east     <= a_west;
            psum_south <= psum_north +
                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};
         end
      end
   end

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// N x N weight-stationary systolic array (the TPU MXU core).
//
// One tpu_pe per grid element, wired as a 2D mesh (per CLAUDE.md: replicated
// hardware via generate, never a procedural unroll):
//
//   - weights load from the top: w_top[j] feeds column j; each column is a
//     vertical shift register (w_south of PE(i,j) -> w_north of PE(i+1,j)).
//   - activations flow west to east: a_left[i] enters row i; a_east of PE(i,j)
//     feeds a_west of PE(i,j+1).
//   - partial sums flow north to south and accumulate; the top row's psum_north
//     is tied to zero (fresh accumulation), and c_bot[j] is the column-j result
//     leaving the bottom row.
//
// With weight B[i][j] held at PE(i,j) and activation A[m][i] entering row i,
// column j computes C[m][j] = sum_i A[m][i] * B[i][j]. Input/output skew (which
// time-aligns rows and columns) lives in the tpu top, not here.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu_array
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N PEs)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	// synchronous, active high
    input		ld,	// weight-load shift enable
    input		en,	// compute enable
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_top,	// weight inputs, one per column (load)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_left,	// activation inputs, one per row
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_bot	// column results, one per column
    );

   // mesh nets: a_h carries activations east, psum_v carries sums south,
   // w_v carries weights south during load.
   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];
   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];
   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];

   genvar	   i, j;
   generate
      // west edge: activation inputs into column 0 of each row
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west
         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];
      end
      // top edge: zero partial sums, weight inputs; bottom edge: results out

      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge

         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];

      end

      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row
         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col
            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe

               .rst        (rst),

               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),
               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));
         end
      end
   endgenerate

endmodule
//#############################################################################
// Copyright: Zero ASIC. All rights Reserved.
// Author: Andreas Olofsson
// License:  MIT (see LICENSE file in LogikBench repository)
//#############################################################################
//
// Weight-stationary systolic matrix-multiply tile (a Google-TPU-style MXU).
//
// Computes one tile product C = A * B, where A and B are N x N signed operands
// (DW bits) and C is N x N signed (ACCW bits). B is the "weight" matrix, loaded
// into and held by the array; A is the "activation" matrix, streamed through; C
// drains out the bottom. Built from a tpu_array of N x N tpu_pe cells.
//
// Operation has two phases the host drives via valids:
//
//   1. Weight load: assert w_valid for N cycles. w_data carries one weight row
//      per cycle, N signed weights packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }. Because the
//      array loads top-down (shift south), push the BOTTOM matrix row first:
//      cycle k carries B[N-1-k][*]. After N cycles PE(i,j) holds B[i][j].
//
//   2. Compute: assert a_valid while streaming A one row per cycle, a_data
//      packed { col N-1 ... col 0 } = A[m][N-1..0]. Row m of the result, C[m][*]
//      (packed the same way), appears on c_data with c_valid high, 2*N-1 cycles
//      after its A row was accepted.
//
// Time alignment: row i of A is delayed i cycles entering the array (input
// skew) so partial sums chain correctly down each column; column j of the
// result is delayed N-1-j cycles (output de-skew) so a full result row leaves
// together. Both skew networks and the valid pipeline advance only with the
// array (compute enable), so the 2*N-1 latency is exact. Per CLAUDE.md the skew
// chains are generated per-row/column shift registers, not a procedural unroll.
//
//#############################################################################

module tpu
  #(parameter N = 8,	 // array dimension (N x N)
    parameter DW = 8,	 // operand width (signed)
    parameter ACCW = 32) // result / accumulator width (signed)
   (
    input		clk,
    input		rst,	 // synchronous, active high
    input		w_valid, // weight-row valid (load phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	w_data,	 // weight row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    input		a_valid, // activation-row valid (compute phase)
    input [N*DW-1:0]	a_data,	 // activation row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    output		c_valid, // result-row valid
    output [N*ACCW-1:0]	c_data	 // result row, packed { col N-1 ... col 0 }
    );

   // total injection-to-output latency, in compute cycles
   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;

   // load weights while w_valid; otherwise the array computes (and drains)
   wire	      ld = w_valid;
   wire	      en = ~w_valid;

   //##########################################################################
   // Input skew: row i of the activation is delayed i cycles. Zero is injected
   // when a_valid is low so idle/drain cycles add nothing to the accumulators.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;
   genvar	   i;
   generate
      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew
         wire [DW-1:0] din =
		       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};
         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;
         end
         else begin : g_skewn
            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  sr[0] <= din;
                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)
                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // The systolic array.
   //##########################################################################
   wire [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot;
   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_array
     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),
      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));

   //##########################################################################
   // Output de-skew: column j is delayed N-1-j cycles so all columns of a
   // result row line up. Column N-1 (longest path) needs no delay.
   //##########################################################################
   genvar j;
   generate
      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew
         localparam D = N - 1 - j;
         wire [ACCW-1:0] cin = c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW];
         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = cin;
         end
         else begin : g_deskewn
            reg [ACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];
            integer k;
            always @(posedge clk) begin
               if (rst) begin
                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= {ACCW{1'b0}};
               end
               else if (en) begin
                  dr[0] <= cin;
                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)
                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];
               end
            end
            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = dr[D-1];
         end
      end
   endgenerate

   //##########################################################################
   // Valid pipeline: c_valid is a_valid delayed LAT compute cycles.
   //##########################################################################
   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;
   always @(posedge clk) begin
      if (rst)
        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};
      else if (en)
        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};
   end
   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];

endmodule

[Step 2/60]
// Self-checking testbench for the tpu benchmark (8x8 weight-stationary
// systolic matmul tile). Reads vectors.dat, one line per clock cycle:
//   w_valid w_data(64b) a_valid a_data(64b)   c_valid c_data(256b)   (hex)
// The expected outputs are the ORIGINAL logikbench tpu's per-cycle behavior;
// c_valid and c_data are compared CYCLE-ACCURATELY every cycle (c_data only
// while c_valid is high — the original holds stale data between results).
module tb;
  localparam N = 8, DW = 8, ACCW = 32;
  int total_checks;
  int total_errors;

  logic clk, rst;
  logic w_valid, a_valid, c_valid;
  logic [N*DW-1:0] w_data, a_data;
  logic [N*ACCW-1:0] c_data;

  tpu dut
    (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .w_valid(w_valid), .w_data(w_data),
     .a_valid(a_valid), .a_data(a_data), .c_valid(c_valid), .c_data(c_data));

  initial clk = 0;
  always #5 clk = ~clk;

  integer fd, rc, line_num;
  string line_buf;
  logic s_wv, s_av, e_cv;
  logic [63:0] s_wd, s_ad;
  logic [255:0] e_cd;

  initial begin
    total_checks = 0;
    total_errors = 0;
    w_valid = 0; a_valid = 0; w_data = 0; a_data = 0;
    rst = 1;
    repeat (4) @(negedge clk);
    rst = 0;

    fd = $fopen("vectors.dat", "r");
    if (fd == 0) begin
      $display("ERROR: cannot open vectors.dat");
      $fatal(1);
    end
    line_num = 0;
    while (!$feof(fd)) begin
      line_num = line_num + 1;
      void'($fgets(line_buf, fd));
      if (line_buf.len() == 0) continue;
      if (line_buf.substr(0, 0) == "#") continue;
      rc = $sscanf(line_buf, "%h %h %h %h %h %h", s_wv, s_wd, s_av, s_ad, e_cv, e_cd);
      if (rc != 6) continue;
      w_valid = s_wv; w_data = s_wd; a_valid = s_av; a_data = s_ad;
      @(negedge clk);
      total_checks = total_checks + 1;
      if (c_valid !== e_cv || (e_cv && c_data !== e_cd)) begin
        $display("TB_ERROR line=%0d exp(cv=%b cd=%h) got(cv=%b cd=%h)",
                 line_num, e_cv, e_cd, c_valid, c_data);
        total_errors = total_errors + 1;
        if (total_errors > 20) begin
          $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
          $fatal(1, "FAIL (too many errors, aborting)");
        end
      end
    end
    $fclose(fd);

    $display("TB_SUMMARY total=%0d errors=%0d", total_checks, total_errors);
    if (total_errors != 0) $fatal(1, "FAIL");
    $display("PASS");
    $finish;
  end
endmodule

[ASSISTANT]
The baseline passes with cost ~4.62M. The critical path goes through the multiplier (8x8→16 bit) and then the 32-bit add in the PE's psum_south. Let me think about optimization strategies:

1. **Optimize the multiplier**: Replace the signed 8x8 multiply with an unsigned multiply + sign correction, or use Booth encoding. The product of two 8-bit signed numbers needs special handling.
2. **Reduce the adder path**: The 32-bit add `psum_north + sign_extend(prod)` is on the critical path. We could use a narrower accumulator or optimize the add structure.
3. **Narrow the accumulator width**: 8×8 signed product is 16 bits, sum of 8 such products max 8+16=24 bits, so we only need ~24-bit accumulators + sign extension, not 32.
4. **Pipeline registers**: The design is already heavily pipelined by the systolic structure.

Let me try narrowing the accumulator to 24 bits internally (8 products of 16-bit each = 24 bits needed), then sign-extending at the output. This reduces the adder width and the deskew register width.

Let me first understand the exact timing. The critical path is: weight register → multiplier → adder → psum_south register. The multiplier is combinational from `a_west` (which comes from the register `a_east` of the PE to the west) and `w` (the stored weight). So the path is actually: `a_east` reg (west PE) → multiply → add → `psum_south` reg. Or: `w` reg → multiply → add → `psum_south` reg.

Let me try narrowing the accumulator. With 8-bit signed inputs, max product = 127*127 = 16129, min = -128*127 = -16256. Sum of 8 max products = 8*16129 = 129032, which fits in 18 bits unsigned. But we need signed: range is about ±130000, which fits in 18 bits signed (±131071). Actually worst case: 8 * 128*128 = 131072 for all -128 * -128. Let me use 18-bit accumulators to be safe, or just use 24 bits to be very safe.

Actually, let me be more careful. The product of two 8-bit signed numbers is 16 bits. The sum of 8 such 16-bit products needs 16+3=19 bits. So a 19-bit accumulator suffices. But I'll use a bit more to be safe and let Yosys optimize.
  -> create_file(content="//#############################################################################\n// Optimized TPU: narrow accumulator (24-bit internal), 8x8 signed multiply\n//#############################################################################\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 24)  // narrowed: 8 * 16-bit product fits in 24 bits\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north +\n                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 24)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; Same cost - the deskew narrowing didn't change the synthesis result much (the sign extension wiring is trivial). The critical path is the multiplier + adder chain. Let me now try a fundamentally different approach: replace the signed multiply with an unsigned multiply and sign correction. The signed multiply `a*b` where a and b are 8-bit can be computed as:

If we let `a_u = a[7:0]` (unsigned), `b_u = b[7:0]` (unsigned), then:
- `prod = a_u * b_u` (16-bit unsigned)
- If `a` is negative (a[7]=1), subtract `b_u << 8` (i.e., subtract 256*b)
- If `b` is negative (b[7]=1), subtract `a_u << 8` (i.e., subtract 256*a)

Actually, a cleaner approach: `signed_mult = unsigned_mult(a_u, b_u) - (a[7] ? b_u << 8 : 0) - (b[7] ? a_u << 8 : 0)`

But this might not help with Yosys since Yosys can already optimize signed multipliers well. Let me instead try a different approach: use a higher target_delay to see if we can reduce area, since the delay is already much higher than the target.

[ASSISTANT]
Same result at 700ps - Yosys is already using the same mapping. The target_delay doesn't help much below 700. Let me now focus on RTL-level optimizations. 

The critical path is: `w` register → multiplier → adder → `psum_south` register. The multiplier (8x8 signed) is the biggest contributor. Let me try an alternative: instead of computing `a_west * w` and adding to `psum_north`, I could try to restructure the multiply to be more efficient.

One approach: Since the accumulator is only 19 bits and the product is 16 bits, I could try splitting the product into upper and lower halves and doing the add differently. But Yosys should already optimize this.

Let me try a completely different approach: instead of the systolic array with per-PE multiply, I could implement a non-systolic (but cycle-equivalent) design that computes all 8 products in parallel per column and sums them with a tree adder. But the cycle-exact behavior with skew/deskew is the key constraint.

Actually, let me think about this differently. The systolic structure means each PE does `psum_south = psum_north + a*w`. The critical path goes through one multiply + one add. If I could somehow break the multiply-add into two stages (pipeline), that would halve the critical path, but it would change the cycle behavior.

Let me instead try to optimize the multiplier itself. An 8x8 signed multiplier can be built as unsigned multiply of the magnitudes with sign correction. Or I could use a Baugh-Wooley multiplier. But Yosis typically uses a good multiplier mapping already.

Let me try a different idea: reduce the multiply to unsigned and handle the sign separately, as this might allow Yosys to use a more efficient multiplier structure:
  -> create_file(content="//#############################################################################\n// Optimized TPU: narrow accumulator (19-bit), unsigned multiply + sign correction\n//#############################################################################\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Unsigned multiply + sign correction\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] uprod = a_west * w;  // unsigned 8x8 = 16 bits\n   wire a_neg = a_west[DW-1];\n   wire w_neg = w[DW-1];\n   // signed product = uprod - (a_neg ? w<<8 : 0) - (w_neg ? a<<8 : 0)\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] corr_a = w_neg ? (a_west << DW) : {2*DW{1'b0}};\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] corr_w = a_neg ? (w << DW) : {2*DW{1'b0}};\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(uprod) - $signed(corr_a) - $signed(corr_w);\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_north +\n                          {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam IACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*IACCW-1:0] c_bot;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(IACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), .c_bot(c_bot));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; [ASSISTANT]
The unsigned multiply approach is worse (3.5M vs 3.24M). Yosys handles signed multiply better than my manual sign correction. Let me go back to the signed multiply version and try other approaches.

Let me think about what's really on the critical path. The path goes from `w` register (QN output) through the multiplier and then a long adder chain to the `psum_south` register. The adder is 19 bits wide. 

Key insight: In the systolic array, the partial sum flows vertically (north to south). The critical path includes `psum_north` (output of PE above) → add → `psum_south` (registered). But the multiply `a_west * w` also feeds into the add. So the path is max(multiply, psum_north) → add → register.

The multiply is from registered inputs (a_east of west PE, and w register), and psum_north is from the register of the PE above. So the critical path is: register → multiply + register → add → register.

Since both the multiply and the psum come from registers, they happen in parallel. The critical path is max(multiply_delay, psum_delay) + add_delay. The multiply is likely the dominant part.

Let me try a different approach: pre-compute the product and register it, then do the add in the next cycle. But that would change the latency. Actually, looking at the systolic structure more carefully, there's already a pipeline: `a_east` is registered (the activation moves one hop per cycle), and `psum_south` is registered. The multiply happens combinationally between the `a_east` register of the west PE and the `psum_south` register.

One idea: register the product before adding. If I add a pipeline register for the product, the multiply and add happen in separate cycles, but this changes the latency. The latency is 2*N-1 = 15 cycles. If I add one pipeline stage in the PE, the latency would change... but actually, the activation also needs to be delayed by one more cycle to match. This is tricky.

Let me instead try to see if I can reduce the adder depth. With 19-bit addition, the adder chain is ~19 deep. Let me try using a carry-lookahead style adder. But Yosys should already optimize this.

Let me try another approach: reduce the accumulator to exactly the minimum needed. With 8-bit signed multiply, max |product| = 128*128 = 16384. Sum of 8 = 131072 = 2^17. So 18 bits unsigned, or 18 bits signed (range -131072 to 131071). But 131072 doesn't fit in 18-bit signed (max is 131071). So 19 bits is the minimum. Let me verify this is truly the minimum needed.

Actually wait - let me reconsider. The products can be positive or negative. The max sum is when all 8 products are the max magnitude with the same sign: 8 * 16384 = 131072. In 19-bit signed, range is -262144 to 262143. So 19 bits is sufficient. Could I use 18 bits? Range -131072 to 131071. The max sum 131072 would overflow! So 19 is indeed the minimum.

Let me now try a completely different strategy: instead of a systolic array with one MAC per PE per cycle, I could try to exploit the fact that the weight is stationary and compute the dot product differently. But the cycle-exact behavior is the constraint.

Let me look at the actual timing path more carefully. The path starts at the `w` register QN output, goes through the multiplier, then the adder, and ends at a deskew register. Wait, the endpoint is `vh_88da09fc5cfd` - let me check if this is a deskew register. The path goes through multiply and add, so it must be the `psum_south` register of the bottom PE row, which feeds the deskew chain.

Actually, looking at the endpoint `vh_88da09fc5cfd`, it could be a deskew register. The path from `w` register through multiply → add → `psum_south` → deskew register. But `psum_south` is registered, so the deskew register would be fed by the registered output, not the combinational path. So the critical path must be from `w` register through multiply and add to the `psum_south` register of the bottom row PE.

Let me try to reduce the multiply-add path. One approach: split the product into lower and upper parts and add them separately to reduce the adder chain:

`psum_south = psum_north + sign_extend(prod)`
= `psum_north + {{3{prod[15]}}, prod}`

The lower 16 bits of prod are added to the lower 16 bits of psum_north, and the upper 3 bits (sign extension) are added to the upper 3 bits. This is just a 19-bit addition, which Yosys handles. 

Let me try a fundamentally different approach - using a simpler unsigned multiply but with a different sign handling that might be cheaper:
  -> create_file(content="//#############################################################################\n// Optimized TPU: narrow accumulator (19-bit), signed multiply, try Booth\n//#############################################################################\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   // Signed product: use $signed multiply\n   wire signed [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   \n   // Partial sum accumulation\n   wire signed [ACCW-1:0] psum_ext = $signed(psum_north);\n   wire signed [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n   \n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= psum_ext + prod_ext;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; Let me think about what the systolic array does. With input skew of i cycles for row i, and output deskew of N-1-j cycles for column j, the PE at (i,j) processes its MAC at cycle `t - (N-1-j) - i` relative to the output. The key constraint is the 15-cycle latency and the stall behavior.

tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t [ASSISTANT]
The carry-save approach fails because the carry-save doesn't work correctly - my CSA implementation is wrong. The issue is that `sum_carry = psum_north + carry_north + prod_ext` followed by splitting into sum and carry doesn't actually implement a proper carry-save adder. A proper CSA needs a full adder: `sum = a ^ b ^ c`, `carry = (a & b) | (a & c) | (b & c)`. But even then, the issue is that the carry-save representation changes the latency/timing of the computation - the carry-save needs to be converted back to binary at the output, and the conversion adds a delay.

Actually, the bigger issue is that my CSA implementation using `+` doesn't properly separate sum and carry. The synthesis tool would just implement it as a regular 3-input add. Let me abandon this approach and go back to the best design.

Let me try a different approach: since the critical path goes through the multiplier and adder, let me see if I can reduce the multiplier complexity. For an 8-bit signed multiply, the result is 16 bits. But the accumulator only needs 19 bits, so the top 3 bits are sign extension. Let me try to decompose the multiply into partial products that can be added more efficiently.

Actually, let me try a simpler optimization: can I remove the reset on some registers? The `a_east` registers don't need reset since they're just passing data through. And the deskew registers get their values from computation that happens after reset. Let me try removing unnecessary resets to reduce the register complexity (reset logic adds area to flip-flops).

Wait, looking at the synthesis, the flip-flops used are `DFFHQNx1` which are DFFs with active-low reset (QN output). The reset is already part of the flop. Removing it might not help.

Let me try a different approach entirely. Let me see if I can use a 4x4 (or other decomposition) multiplier to reduce the critical path. An 8-bit multiply can be decomposed as:
```
a = aH.aL (4 bits each)
b = bH.bL (4 bits each)
prod = aH*bH << 8 + (aH*bL + aL*bH) << 4 + aL*bL
```

This uses four 4x4 multiplies and three additions. The 4x4 multipliers are much smaller and faster. But this might not help with Yosys since it already decomposes multipliers.

Same result at 300ps. Yosis seems to hit a floor with this design structure. The delay is 819ps regardless of target_delay below 700ps. The area stays at 3948.

Let me try a radically different approach. The key observation is that the critical path goes through the full 8x8 multiplier followed by a 19-bit add. What if I break the multiplier into partial products and fold them into the accumulator more efficiently?

Idea: Instead of computing `prod = a * w` and then `psum + prod`, I could compute the partial products of `a * w` and add them to the accumulator one at a time, using a Wallace-tree-like structure. But this is what Yosys does internally for the multiplier.

Let me try a different structural approach: split the 8x8 multiply into two 4x8 multiplies and add them separately, reducing the multiplier depth:

`a * w = (a[7:4] << 4 + a[3:0]) * w = (a[7:4] * w) << 4 + a[3:0] * w`

Each 4x8 multiply is smaller. But this increases the total area (two multiplies instead of one) and the addition is the same.

Actually, let me try another idea: use a Baugh-Wooley multiplier structure which is specifically designed for signed multiplication and might have a shorter critical path than the generic signed multiplier Yosys synthesizes.

Actually, let me step back and think about what could really help. The critical path is 820ps. The area is 3948. The cost is 3.24M. 

Key insight: the critical path goes through ONE PE's multiply-add chain. The 64 PEs are all the same, so the area is dominated by 64 × (8-bit multiply + 19-bit add + 3 registers). If I can reduce the per-PE area or delay, the cost improves linearly.

Let me try: what if I make the accumulator only 17 bits? Wait, I showed earlier that 19 is the minimum. But what about 18? The max sum is 8 * 128*128 = 131072 = 2^17. It requires all 8 inputs to be -128 (for row) and -128 (for weight), giving (-128)*(-128) = 16384, times 8 = 131072. This is a valid case! So 18 bits would overflow.

[ASSISTANT]
The synthesis result is always the same regardless of target_delay (it's already using the minimum-delay mapping). Let me try a fundamentally different RTL approach.

Key idea: What if I avoid the long adder chain in the psum path by splitting the accumulator into two parts - a "fast" lower part and a "slow" upper part with a pipeline register for the carry? This is essentially a carry-save accumulator, but implemented correctly.

The proper way: use a full-adder-based CSA tree. For `psum_north + prod`, instead of a carry-propagate adder, I use a CSA that outputs (sum, carry) in carry-save form. The carry-propagate add only happens at the final output (in the deskew stage).

Let me implement this correctly using explicit full-adder logic:
  -> create_file(content="//#############################################################################\n// Optimized TPU: narrow accumulator (19-bit), proper carry-save accumulation\n//#############################################################################\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,   // carry-save sum part\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  carry_north,  // carry-save carry part (shifted left 1)\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south,  // carry-save sum part\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] carry_south  // carry-save carry part (shifted left 1)\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // Full-adder based CSA: inputs = psum_north, carry_north, prod_ext\n   // sum[i]   = a[i] ^ b[i] ^ c[i]\n   // carry[i] = (a[i] & b[i]) | (a[i] & c[i]) | (b[i] & c[i])\n   // carry is registered shifted left by 1 (carry[0] = 0, carry[i+1] = fa_carry[i])\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry;\n   \n   assign csa_sum[0]   = psum_north[0] ^ carry_north[0] ^ prod_ext[0];\n   assign csa_carry[0] = 1'b0; // no carry into bit 0\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n     for (b = 1; b < ACCW; b = b + 1) begin : g_fa\n       wire a_bit = psum_north[b];\n       wire c_bit = carry_north[b];\n       wire p_bit = prod_ext[b];\n       wire prev_carry = (psum_north[b-1] & carry_north[b-1]) |\n                         (psum_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]) |\n                         (carry_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]);\n       assign csa_sum[b]   = a_bit ^ c_bit ^ p_bit;\n       assign csa_carry[b] = prev_carry;\n     end\n   endgenerate\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         carry_south<= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= csa_sum;\n            carry_south<= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot_sum,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot_carry\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] carry_v[0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot_sum[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n         assign c_bot_carry[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = carry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .carry_north(carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .carry_south(carry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam IACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*IACCW-1:0] c_bot_sum;\n   wire [N*IACCW-1:0] c_bot_carry;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(IACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), \n      .c_bot_sum(c_bot_sum), .c_bot_carry(c_bot_carry));\n\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [IACCW-1:0] cin_sum   = c_bot_sum[j*IACCW +: IACCW];\n         wire [IACCW-1:0] cin_carry = c_bot_carry[j*IACCW +: IACCW];\n         // Final carry-propagate add\n         wire [IACCW-1:0] result = cin_sum + cin_carry;\n         \n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-IACCW){result[IACCW-1]}}, result};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [IACCW-1:0] dr [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= {IACCW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr[0] <= result;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1)\n                    dr[k] <= dr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-IACCW){dr[D-1][IACCW-1]}}, dr[D-1]};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_csa2.v')

[ASSISTANT]
Excellent! The carry-save approach works! Cost dropped from 3.24M to 2.93M (delay 670ps, area 4369). The CSA eliminates the carry-propagate chain in each PE, replacing it with a simple full-adder (one level of logic). The carry-propagate add only happens once at the output.

Now the critical path seems to be in the deskew/output stage (the final carry-propagate add + deskew register chain). Let me optimize further.

Key observations:
1. The CSA approach broke the long adder chain in the PEs, reducing delay significantly
2. Area increased (4369 vs 3948) because of the extra carry registers (19 bits per PE × 64 PEs)
3. The critical path is now in the output stage - the carry-propagate add + deskew

Let me see if I can reduce the carry-propagate add delay by moving it after the deskew (so the deskew registers carry the carry-save form, and the final add happens at the very end). This would make the deskew registers carry both sum and carry, doubling their area, but the adder would only be 19 bits wide and might not be on the critical path.

Actually, looking at the timing path, it ends at a deskew register, so the carry-propagate add IS on the critical path. Let me move the CPA to after the deskew:
  -> create_file(content="//#############################################################################\n// Optimized TPU: carry-save accumulation, CPA after deskew\n//#############################################################################\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // Full-adder based CSA\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry;\n   \n   assign csa_sum[0]   = psum_north[0] ^ carry_north[0] ^ prod_ext[0];\n   assign csa_carry[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n     for (b = 1; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .carry_north(carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .carry_south(carry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam IACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= {DW{1'b0}};\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  sr[0] <= din;\n                  for (k = 1; k < i; k = k + 1)\n                    sr[k] <= sr[k-1];\n               end\n            end\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = sr[i-1];\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   wire [N*IACCW-1:0] c_bot_sum;\n   wire [N*IACCW-1:0] c_bot_carry;\n   tpu_array #(.N(N), .DW(DW), .ACCW(IACCW)) u_array\n     (.clk(clk), .rst(rst), .ld(ld), .en(en),\n      .w_top(w_data), .a_left(a_left), \n      .c_bot_sum(c_bot_sum), .c_bot_carry(c_bot_carry));\n\n   // Deskew both sum and carry, then do CPA\n   genvar j;\n   generate\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_deskew\n         localparam D = N - 1 - j;\n         wire [IACCW-1:0] cin_sum   = c_bot_sum[j*IACCW +: IACCW];\n         wire [IACCW-1:0] cin_carry = c_bot_carry[j*IACCW +: IACCW];\n         \n         if (D == 0) begin : g_deskew0\n            // No deskew, direct CPA\n            wire [IACCW-1:0] result = cin_sum + cin_carry;\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-IACCW){result[IACCW-1]}}, result};\n         end\n         else begin : g_deskewn\n            reg [IACCW-1:0] dr_sum   [0:D-1];\n            reg [IACCW-1:0] dr_carry [0:D-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0; k < D; k = k + 1) begin\n                    dr_sum[k]   <= {IACCW{1'b0}};\n                    dr_carry[k] <= {IACCW{1'b0}};\n                  end\n               end\n               else if (en) begin\n                  dr_sum[0]   <= cin_sum;\n                  dr_carry[0] <= cin_carry;\n                  for (k = 1; k < D; k = k + 1) begin\n                    dr_sum[k]   <= dr_sum[k-1];\n                    dr_carry[k] <= dr_carry[k-1];\n                  end\n               end\n            end\n            // CPA after deskew\n            wire [IACCW-1:0] result = dr_sum[D-1] + dr_carry[D-1];\n            assign c_data[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = {{(ACCW-IACCW){result[IACCW-1]}}, result};\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\n   reg [LAT-1:0] vpipe;\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst)\n        vpipe <= {LAT{1'b0}};\n      else if (en)\n        vpipe <= {vpipe[LAT-2:0], a_valid};\n   end\n   assign c_valid = vpipe[LAT-1];\n\nendmodule\n", filename='design_csa3.v')

Moving the CPA after deskew increased the area (4630 vs 4369) and slightly reduced delay (657 vs 670), giving a worse cost (3.04M vs 2.93M). The extra deskew registers for the carry outweigh the benefit. Let me go back to design_csa2.v (CPA before deskew) which is the best so far.

Now let me think about what else I can optimize. The critical path in design_csa2 was: multiplier → CSA (one full-adder level) → register, then CPA (19-bit add) → deskew register. The CPA is on the critical path at the output.

Wait, actually looking at the design_csa2 timing path more carefully, the endpoint is `vh_f9da47edf15f` which is likely a deskew register. The path goes through multiplier → CSA → psum_south register, then (combinational) CPA → deskew register. So the critical path is: register (a_east or w) → multiply → CSA → psum_south register → CPA → deskew register. But that's two register stages! The CPA must be in the deskew path.

Actually, let me re-examine: in design_csa2, the CPA is done combinationally before the deskew register: `dr[0] <= result` where `result = cin_sum + cin_carry`. So the path is: psum_south register → CPA (19-bit add) → deskew register. This is a separate path from the multiply/CSA path.

The critical path is the longer of:
1. w/a register → multiply → CSA → psum_south register
2. psum_south register → CPA → deskew register

With the CSA, path 1 is shorter (no carry chain), and path 2 (CPA) is now the bottleneck.

To fix this, I could register the CPA output before the deskew. That is, add a pipeline register for the CPA result. But that would change the latency by 1 cycle. Wait, let me think about this...

Actually, the CPA happens at the bottom of the array. The result then goes through D deskew stages. If I add a register for the CPA, the total delay is D+1 stages instead of D. This would change the latency.

Hmm, but the latency is fixed at 2*N-1 = 15 cycles. The deskew depth is D = N-1-j for column j. The total path from PE output to c_data is D stages. If I add a CPA register, it becomes D+1 stages, which changes the output timing.

Unless I reduce the deskew depth by 1 to compensate. But then the timing wouldn't be correct for all columns.

Let me think differently. What if I do the CPA in a pipelined fashion? Or what if I reduce the CPA width? Actually, let me check: the carry-save format means the sum and carry are 19 bits each. The CPA is a 19-bit add. But the carry bits are mostly zero (only bits 1-16 can have carries from the product). Actually, the carry from the CSA can be nonzero for all 19 bits.

Let me try a different approach: instead of a full CPA before deskew, let me try to combine the CPA into the first deskew stage. That is, the first deskew register computes `result = sum + carry` and registers it. This is what design_csa2 already does. The critical path is this 19-bit add → register.

What if I use a faster adder? Let me try a prefix adder (like the Brent-Kung or Sklansky from the reference). But Yosis should already optimize this.

Let me try another idea: reduce the CSA width. Since the product is 16 bits, and the sign extension is only 3 bits (to 19), the upper 3 bits of the CSA are simpler. The CPA for the upper bits is just adding the sign extension, which is trivial.

Actually, let me try a completely different idea: what if I don't use CSA but instead pipeline the multiply and add separately? That is, register the product, then add it to the psum in the next cycle. This adds one cycle of latency but... the systolic structure means each PE already takes one cycle. Adding a pipeline register would double the latency per PE, which changes the 2*N-1 timing.

Hmm, that won't work directly. Let me instead try to reduce the CSA area. The CSA has 19 full adders per PE. The product is 16 bits, sign-extended to 19. [ASSISTANT]
The output is `00020000` = 0x20000 = 131072 = 8 * (-128) * (-128) = 8 * 16384. So 131072 IS tested! This confirms that 18-bit signed (max 131071) is NOT enough - we need 19 bits (max 262143). So 19 bits is indeed the minimum.

Now let me focus on optimizing the CPA. Since 131072 = 0x20000, it needs 18 bits (bit 17 is set). In 19-bit signed, this is positive (bit 18 = 0). So the CPA needs at least 19 bits.

Let me try a different approach: instead of the full-adder-based CSA, use a simpler CSA structure. The CSA takes 3 inputs: psum_north, carry_north, prod_ext. But I can use a different formulation.

Actually, let me think about this differently. The CSA has per-bit logic: `sum = a ^ b ^ c`, `carry = maj(a,b,c)`. This is one level of logic per bit. The critical path in the CSA is just one full-adder deep (all bits computed in parallel). Then the carry is registered. So the CSA itself is fast.

The bottleneck is the 19-bit CPA at the output. Let me try to speed up the CPA by using a carry-lookahead structure.

But first, let me try the simpler optimization of reducing the CSA and carry register width. The product is 16 bits, sign-extended to 19. The upper 3 bits (bits 16-18) of the product are all the sign bit. For these upper bits, the CSA is:
- bit 16: sum = psum[16] ^ carry[16] ^ sign, carry = maj(psum[16], carry[16], sign)
- bit 17: same
- bit 18: same

These are simpler because the sign bit is just one bit. But Yosis should already optimize this.

Let me try a different approach: reduce the internal accumulator to 17 bits (16-bit product + 1 carry bit) and see if the overflow case still passes. With 17 bits, the max is 65535 (unsigned) or ±65536 (signed). 131072 needs 18 bits. So 17 bits would definitely overflow. Not an option.

Let me try another idea: what if I keep the CSA but make the carry register narrower? The carry from bit 15 (the MSB of the product) propagates to bits 16, 17, 18. The carry from the upper bits (16, 17) also propagates. So I need the full 19-bit carry.

Let me try a completely different optimization: reduce the number of PEs. Currently we have 64 PEs (8x8). But each PE has a multiplier, which is expensive. What if we time-share multipliers? But the systolic structure requires one MAC per PE per cycle, so we can't reduce the number of PEs without changing the cycle behavior.

Let me try yet another approach: use a simpler multiplier structure. Instead of a full 8x8 multiplier, use a Booth encoder or a split multiplier. But Yosis already optimizes the multiplier.

Let me try the approach of using a different target_delay with the CSA design:
  -> run_evaluation(filename='design_csa2.v', target_delay=700)

[ASSISTANT]
target_delay=555 is still the best at 2.84M. Now let me try to optimize the RTL structure further. The critical path goes through the multiplier and CSA. Let me look at what happens if I reduce the CSA width by using a narrower product. 

Actually, looking at the timing path for the best result, it goes through: w register → multiplier (XNOR/XOR gates) → CSA (MAJI gates) → register → CPA (A2O1A1O1I, O2A1O1I gates) → deskew register. The path spans two pipeline stages (register → combinational → register → combinational → register), so the critical path is the longer of the two combinational segments.

Let me focus on the RTL optimizations now. One key idea: the product `a * w` is 16 bits, but the CSA only needs to handle 16 bits + sign extension. What if I reduce the CSA to only 16 bits and handle the sign extension separately? The upper bits of the accumulator are just sign extension + carry propagation, which can be handled more efficiently.

Actually, let me try a different approach: reduce the number of full adders by noting that the product's upper 3 bits are just sign extension. For the upper 3 bits, the CSA input for prod_ext is constant (all sign bits), so the full adder simplifies:

For bit i where prod_ext[i] = sign (0 or 1):
- sum[i] = psum[i] ^ carry[i] ^ sign
- carry[i+1] = (psum[i] & carry[i]) | (psum[i] & sign) | (carry[i] & sign)

If sign = 1: sum[i] = ~(psum[i] ^ carry[i]), carry[i+1] = psum[i] | carry[i]
If sign = 0: sum[i] = psum[i] ^ carry[i], carry[i+1] = psum[i] & carry[i]

This is simpler but Yosis should already optimize it. Let me try a different structural change.

Let me try reducing the internal accumulator to 18 bits. We established that 131072 needs 19 bits, but what if I handle the overflow bit separately? Or what if I just accept that the all-(-128) case needs 19 bits and keep 19.

Let me instead try to make the carry-save approach more efficient by reducing the carry register width. The carry from bit i goes to bit i+1. The product is 16 bits (bits 0-15), and the sign extension is bits 16-18. The carry chain goes:
- Bits 0-15: carry from product computation
- Bit 16: carry from bit 15 (can be non-zero)
- Bits 17-18: carry from bits 16-17

So I need the full 19-bit carry. But the upper carries are simpler (only sign + psum + carry, no product).

Let me try a different optimization: instead of the full-adder CSA, use a simpler half-adder based approach for the upper bits. Or better yet, let me try removing the reset on the carry registers (they don't need reset since they'll be zeroed by the computation when en is active and the top row has zero carry):

[ASSISTANT]
The non-CSA design doesn't benefit from target_delay changes (it's already at minimum delay). The CSA design is clearly better. Let me now copy the CSA design as the main design and continue optimizing:
  -> replace_file(filename='design.v', content="//#############################################################################\n// Optimized TPU: carry-save accumulation, narrow accumulator (19-bit)\n//#############################################################################\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  carry_north,\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // Full-adder based CSA: inputs = psum_north, carry_north, prod_ext\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry;\n   \n   assign csa_sum[0]   = psum_north[0] ^ carry_north[0] ^ prod_ext[0];\n   assign csa_carry[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n     for (b = 1; b < ACCW; b = b + 1) begin : g_fa\n       wire a_bit = psum_north[b];\n       wire c_bit = carry_north[b];\n       wire p_bit = prod_ext[b];\n       wire prev_carry = (psum_north[b-1] & carry_north[b-1]) |\n                         (psum_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]) |\n                         (carry_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]);\n       assign csa_sum[b]   = a_bit ^ c_bit ^ p_bit;\n       assign csa_carry[b] = prev_carry;\n     end\n   endgenerate\n\n   always @(posedge clk) begin\n      if (rst) begin\n         w          <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         a_east     <= {DW{1'b0}};\n         psum_south <= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         carry_south<= {ACCW{1'b0}};\n      end\n      else begin\n         if (ld)\n           w <= w_north;\n         if (en) begin\n            a_east     <= a_west;\n            psum_south <= csa_sum;\n            carry_south<= csa_carry;\n         end\n      end\n   end\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu_array\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tld,\n    input\t\ten,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_top,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_left,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_bot_sum,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0] c_bot_carry\n    );\n\n   wire [DW-1:0]   a_h    [0:N-1][0:N];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] psum_v [0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] carry_v[0:N][0:N-1];\n   wire [DW-1:0]   w_v    [0:N][0:N-1];\n\n   genvar\t   i, j;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_west\n         assign a_h[i][0] = a_left[i*DW +: DW];\n      end\n      for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_edge\n         assign psum_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign carry_v[0][j] = {ACCW{1'b0}};\n         assign w_v[0][j]    = w_top[j*DW +: DW];\n         assign c_bot_sum[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = psum_v[N][j];\n         assign c_bot_carry[j*ACCW +: ACCW] = carry_v[N][j];\n      end\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_pe_row\n         for (j = 0; j < N; j = j + 1) begin : g_pe_col\n            tpu_pe #(.DW(DW), .ACCW(ACCW)) u_pe\n              (.clk        (clk),\n               .rst        (rst),\n               .ld         (ld),\n               .en         (en),\n               .w_north    (w_v[i][j]),\n               .a_west     (a_h[i][j]),\n               .psum_north (psum_v[i][j]),\n               .carry_north(carry_v[i][j]),\n               .w_south    (w_v[i+1][j]),\n               .a_east     (a_h[i][j+1]),\n               .psum_south (psum_v[i+1][j]),\n               .carry_south(carry_v[i+1][j]));\n         end\n      end\n   endgenerate\n\nendmodule\n\nmodule tpu\n  #(parameter N = 8,\n    parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 32)\n   (\n    input\t\tclk,\n    input\t\trst,\n    input\t\tw_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\tw_data,\n    input\t\ta_valid,\n    input [N*DW-1:0]\ta_data,\n    output\t\tc_valid,\n    output [N*ACCW-1:0]\tc_data\n    );\n\n   localparam LAT = 2*N - 1;\n   localparam IACCW = 19;\n\n   wire\t      ld = w_valid;\n   wire\t      en = ~w_valid;\n\n   wire [N*DW-1:0] a_left;\n   genvar\t   i;\n   generate\n      for (i = 0; i < N; i = i + 1) begin : g_skew\n         wire [DW-1:0] din =\n\t\t       a_valid ? a_data[i*DW +: DW] : {DW{1'b0}};\n         if (i == 0) begin : g_skew0\n            assign a_left[i*DW +: DW] = din;\n         end\n         else begin : g_skewn\n            reg [DW-1:0] sr [0:i-1];\n            integer k;\n            always @(posedge clk) begin\n               if (rst) begin\n                  for (k = 0;
