[ASSISTANT]
Good, 2.84M confirmed. Now let me try to reduce the area of the CSA design. The area is 4363, which is higher than the non-CSA design (3948). The extra area comes from the carry registers (19 bits × 64 PEs = 1216 extra flip-flops) plus the CSA logic.

Let me try to reduce the CSA area. One approach: only carry-save the lower 16 bits (the product width), and use a simple add for the upper 3 bits. This reduces the carry register from 19 to 16 bits:

Actually, let me think about this more carefully. The CSA needs to handle the full 19-bit width because the carry propagates through all bits. If I only CSA the lower 16 bits, the upper 3 bits need a separate carry-propagate add, which would still be on the critical path.

Let me try a different approach: reduce the CSA register width by not storing the carry for the upper sign-extension bits. For bits 16-18, the product is just the sign bit. The CSA for these bits is:
- sum[16] = psum[16] ^ carry[16] ^ sign
- carry[17] = maj(psum[16], carry[16], sign)

Since sign is the same for bits 16, 17, 18, I could potentially optimize this. But the carry still propagates.

Let me try yet another approach: remove the reset on the carry and psum registers to save area (reset logic adds to flip-flop cost). Since the testbench asserts reset before the stream, but the registers will be loaded with meaningful data during computation, the reset might not be needed for the psum/carry registers:

Actually, looking at the synthesized FFs, they're all `DFFHQNx1_ASAP7_75t_R` which have a reset input. Whether I use it or not, the FF has the same area. So removing reset won't help.

Let me try a different structural optimization. The key insight is that the carry-save approach adds 19 bits of carry registers per PE. If I could reduce this, the area would decrease.

What if I use a hybrid approach: carry-save for the lower bits (where the adder chain is long) and carry-propagate for the upper bits (where it's short)? For example, CSA for bits 0-15 (16 bits) and CPA for bits 16-18 (3 bits). The CPA for 3 bits is just a 3-bit adder, which is fast.

Let me try this:
  -> create_file(content="//#############################################################################\n// Optimized TPU: hybrid CSA (16-bit carry-save + 3-bit carry-propagate)\n//#############################################################################\n\nmodule tpu_pe\n  #(parameter DW = 8,\n    parameter ACCW = 19,\n    parameter CSAW = 16)  // CSA width for lower bits\n   (\n    input\t\t  clk,\n    input\t\t  rst,\n    input\t\t  ld,\n    input\t\t  en,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  w_north,\n    input [DW-1:0]\t  a_west,\n    input [ACCW-1:0]\t  psum_north,\n    input [CSAW-1:0]\t  carry_north,  // carry-save carry for lower bits only\n    output [DW-1:0]\t  w_south,\n    output reg [DW-1:0]\t  a_east,\n    output reg [ACCW-1:0] psum_south,\n    output reg [CSAW-1:0] carry_south\n    );\n\n   reg [DW-1:0] w;\n   assign w_south = w;\n\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] prod = $signed(a_west) * $signed(w);\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] prod_ext = {{(ACCW-2*DW){prod[2*DW-1]}}, prod};\n\n   // Lower bits: CSA (full-adder based)\n   wire [CSAW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [CSAW-1:0] csa_carry;\n   \n   assign csa_sum[0]   = psum_north[0] ^ carry_north[0] ^ prod_ext[0];\n   assign csa_carry[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n     for (b = 1; [ASSISTANT]
The hybrid approach has a bug - the upper bits' carry-propagate add doesn't account for the carry-out of the lower CSA correctly. The issue is in how I combine the upper and lower results. Let me fix this.

Looking at the error: expected `00002c27ffffd752...` but got `00002c27fffcd752...`. The upper bits are wrong. The problem is in the upper CPA computation - the carry from the CSA needs to be properly added.

Actually, the fundamental issue is that the upper 3 bits need the carry from the CSA (bit 15 carry) plus the psum upper bits plus the prod upper bits. My current implementation does `{upper_psum, csa_carry[CSAW-1]} + {upper_prod, 1'b0}`, which is a 4-bit add. But this is wrong because the carry should be from the CSA of the PREVIOUS PE's computation, not the current one.

Wait, no. The CSA computes `psum_north + carry_north + prod_ext` where the carry is the CSA carry from the PE above. The upper bits of the CSA (bits 16-18) should be in carry-save form too, but I'm doing them in CPA form. This means the upper bits need the carry from the lower CSA, and this carry is NOT registered - it's combinational within the same PE. That's fine for the upper bits since they're CPA anyway.

But the issue is that the carry from the upper CPA needs to be stored, not just the CSA carry from the lower bits. Let me reconsider the design.

Actually, the problem is simpler than I think. In the hybrid approach, the upper 3 bits are computed with a carry-propagate add: `upper_psum + upper_prod + csa_carry[15]`. This gives a 4-bit result (3 bits + carry out). The result's 3 bits go into psum_south[18:16], and the carry out should go... where? In the full CSA, the carry would go to the next PE. But in the hybrid, there's no upper carry register. The carry out is lost!

The issue is that the carry from the upper CPA needs to propagate to the next PE. But if I store it in the psum (by sign-extending), it would work. Let me think...

Actually, the problem is that the upper CPA produces a carry-out that gets lost. In the full CSA design, this carry is stored in the carry register and propagated to the next PE. In the hybrid design, I need to handle this carry somehow.

One fix: just store the upper carry in the carry register too. So carry_south would be CSAW+1 bits: the lower CSAW carries plus the upper carry-out.

But this is getting complicated. Let me abandon the hybrid approach and go back to the full CSA design. Let me try other optimizations instead.

Let me try reducing the skew register reset, or try a different multiplier structure. Actually, let me try the key idea: use `a * w` with $signed but force the synthesis to use a specific multiplier structure. Let me try computing the product as partial products:

Actually, let me try something different. The timing path in the best design goes through the multiplier (many XNOR/XOR gates for the partial product generation and compression) and then the CSA (MAJI gates) and then the CPA (A2O1A1O1I, O2A1O1I gates). The multiplier part is the longest.

Let me try to see if using a simpler multiply representation helps. What if I use `a_west * w` without $signed (unsigned multiply) and handle the sign separately in a simpler way? 

Actually wait - let me look at the timing path more carefully. The path starts at the w register (QN output), goes through the multiplier, CSA, and register, then through the CPA and deskew register. So it spans TWO pipeline stages. The total delay is 650ps across two stages. If I can reduce the delay of either stage, the total improves.

Let me try removing the CPA from the deskew path by registering the CPA result. I.e., add a pipeline register for the CPA. This would increase the latency by 1, which I'd need to compensate by reducing the deskew depth by 1.

Wait, the deskew depth is D = N-1-j for column j. The CPA adds delay before the deskew register. If I register the CPA, the total delay becomes D+1 stages. To keep the latency the same, I'd need to reduce D by 1. But D varies per column, and the minimum D is 0 (for column N-1). For column N-1, the CPA result goes directly to the output. If I add a register, column N-1 would have 1 stage instead of 0, changing the timing.

This is tricky. Let me think of another approach.

What if I split the CPA into two stages? A 19-bit adder can be split into two ~10-bit adders with a pipeline register in between. But again, this changes the latency.

Actually, let me try a completely different idea: what if I move the CPA before the deskew, but use a faster adder? Yosis should already use a fast adder, but maybe I can force a prefix adder structure.

Or, let me try reducing the CPA width. The carry-save format means the sum and carry are 19 bits each. But the carry is mostly zeros in the upper bits. The CPA is `sum + carry`. If the carry's upper 3 bits (16-18) are always zero (because the product is 16 bits and the sign extension doesn't generate carries), then I only need a 16-bit CPA.

Wait, but the carry CAN be nonzero in the upper bits due to the carry chain. Let me think: the CSA adds (psum, carry, prod) where prod is sign-extended to 19 bits. The carry from bit 15 (the MSB of the product) can be nonzero, and this carry propagates to bit 16, which generates a carry to bit 17, etc. So the upper carries can be nonzero.

But the carry register's upper 3 bits ARE the carries from the CSA. So in the CPA at the output, I'm adding `sum[18:0] + carry[18:0]`. The carry[18:16] could be nonzero. So I need the full 19-bit CPA.

Let me try a completely different approach: instead of CPA at the output, use a second level of CSA. That is, use a 3:2 CSA at the output to combine sum and carry into a new (sum', carry') pair, then do a final CPA. But that would add another cycle of delay.

Actually, let me go back to the design and try to reduce the area. The area is 4363. Let me see what's contributing to it:
- 64 PEs × (8-bit weight reg + 8-bit activation reg + 19-bit psum reg + 19-bit carry reg + 8×8 multiplier + 19 full adders)
- Skew registers: 0+1+2+...+7 = 28 8-bit registers
- Deskew registers: 7+6+5+4+3+2+1+0 = 28 19-bit registers
- Valid pipeline: 15 bits

The carry registers add 64×19 = 1216 flip-flops. The psum registers are 64×19 = 1216. The weight and activation registers are 64×8 = 512 each. The deskew adds 28×19 = 532. Skew adds 28×8 = 224.

Total FFs: 1216 + 1216 + 512 + 512 + 532 + 224 + 15 = 4227 FFs. At ~0.15 area per FF, that's ~634 area just for FFs.

The multipliers: 64 × 8-bit signed. An 8×8 signed multiplier is about 50-60 gates. At ~0.5 area per gate, that's ~25-30 per multiplier, 1600-1920 total.

The CSA logic: 64 × 19 full adders. Each full adder is ~5 gates. That's ~6080 gates, ~3040 area. Hmm, that seems too high. But wait, Yosis might optimize the CSA to share logic with the multiplier.

Actually, the total area is 4363, so the breakdown seems reasonable. Let me focus on reducing the CSA area. 

One idea: instead of 19 full adders per PE, use fewer. The product is 16 bits, sign-extended to 19. The upper 3 bits (16-18) of prod_ext are all the same (sign bit). For these bits, the full adder simplifies:
- If sign = 0: sum = psum ^ carry, carry_out = psum & carry (half adder)
- If sign = 1: sum = ~(psum ^ carry), carry_out = psum | carry (this is more complex)

But the sign depends on the product, which varies. So I can't simplify these at synthesis time. However, Yosis might already optimize this.

Let me try a different approach: reduce the internal accumulator width to 18 bits and handle the overflow case (131072) by using modular arithmetic. Actually, 131072 = 2^17, so in 18-bit signed (range -131072 to 131071), 131072 is represented as -131072 (overflow). But the expected output is 131072 = 0x20000. In 32-bit, this is 0x00020000. So I'd need to detect the overflow and adjust.

This is risky and complex. Let me try a simpler approach.

Let me try to reduce the area by using a shared multiplier structure. Instead of 64 individual 8×8 multipliers, what if I use a time-multiplexed approach? But the systolic structure requires all 64 PEs to compute simultaneously, so I can't time-multiplex.

Let me try another idea: reduce the activation register width. The `a_east` register is 8 bits, used to delay the activation by one cycle as it passes through the PE. This is 64×8 = 512 FFs. If I could eliminate it, I'd save area. But the systolic structure requires the activation to be delayed by one cycle per PE hop. Without the register, the activation would pass through combinationally, changing the timing.

Actually, wait - what if I combine the activation register with the skew logic? The input skew already delays the activation by i cycles for row i. The per-PE a_east registers add 8 more cycles of delay (one per PE hop across columns). These are necessary for the systolic timing.

Let me try yet another approach: remove the reset from the a_east and carry registers (since they're not needed for correctness if the computation initializes them properly). But as I noted, the FF area doesn't change with/without reset.

Let me try a different structural optimization: instead of the explicit CSA full adders, use Yosis's built-in CSA optimization by writing the add differently. What if I write the PE accumulation as:
```
psum_south <= psum_north + carry_north + prod_ext;
carry_south <= ???
```

This doesn't work because I can't extract the carry from a `+` operation in synthesizable RTL. Let me try another formulation.

Actually, let me try removing the CSA entirely and instead pipelining the multiply-add in the PE. The idea: register the product, then add it to the psum in the next cycle. This adds one cycle of latency per PE row, but the multiply and add are in separate cycles, reducing the critical path.

But this changes the latency! The systolic array's latency is 2*N-1 = 15 cycles, which is the sum of:
- N-1 cycles for activation to propagate across the array (skew + PE hops)
- N cycles for partial sum to propagate down the array
- N-1 cycles for output deskew

Wait, actually the latency is:
- Input skew: N-1 cycles (row N-1 is delayed by N-1)
- PE computation: N cycles (psum flows through N rows)
- Output deskew: N-1 cycles (column 0 is delayed by N-1)
Total: (N-1) + N + (N-1) = 3N-2 = 22? No, that's not right.

Actually, looking at the original design: the latency is 2*N-1 = 15. This is because:
- The first activation enters row 0 at cycle 0 (after skew)
- The psum reaches the bottom of the array after N cycles
- The output deskew adds N-1 cycles for column 0 (but N-1 for column 0 and 0 for column N-1)
- The result for row 0 appears 2*N-1 cycles after its activation was accepted

If I add a pipeline stage in each PE (register the product), the latency would increase by N (one extra cycle per row of PEs), making it 3*N-1 = 23. This doesn't match the required 2*N-1 = 15. So I'd need to compensate by reducing the deskew, which doesn't work.

Actually, let me think about this differently. The psum flows down through N PEs in a column. If each PE adds one extra cycle of latency (for the product register), the psum would take 2N cycles to traverse the column instead of N. The total latency would be (N-1) + 2N + (N-1) = 4N-2 = 30, which is way too much.

So pipelining within the PE doesn't work for this systolic structure.

Let me go back to trying different target_delay values with the CSA design, and also try to optimize the RTL in small ways.

[ASSISTANT]
Now let me try a different approach to reduce area. Instead of carrying the full 19-bit carry through the CSA chain, let me try using a smaller carry width. The key insight is that the product only occupies 16 bits. The upper 3 bits (17-18) of the carry come only from sign extension propagation. If I only CSA the lower 16 bits and handle the upper 3 bits with a simple CPA within the PE (storing the result in the psum), this might reduce the carry register width from 19 to 16 bits.

The trick is that the upper CPA carry-out needs to be stored somewhere. I'll store it as part of the psum (by properly computing the upper bits).

Let me think about this more carefully. For the upper 3 bits:
- psum_upper (3 bits) + carry_from_lower (1 bit) + prod_upper (3 bits, all sign) = result (3 bits) + carry_out (1 bit)

The carry_out from the upper bits represents the sign overflow. In a 19-bit signed accumulator, the carry_out from bit 18 is the overflow, which shouldn't happen (we have enough headroom). So I can safely ignore it.

So the approach is:
1. CSA for bits 0-15 (16 bits): compute csa_sum[0:15] and csa_carry[0:15]
2. CPA for bits 16-18 (3 bits): psum[16:18] + carry[16:18] + prod_ext[16:18] + csa_carry[15]

Wait, but the carry from the CSA (csa_carry[15]) needs to propagate into the upper CPA. And the carry input to the upper CPA from the previous PE's computation needs to be stored. This is the carry_north[16:18] for the upper bits.

The issue is that in the hybrid approach, the upper 3 bits are in CPA form (stored in psum), but the carry from the CPA needs to be passed to the next PE. The carry-out of the 3-bit CPA is a single bit (bit 19), which I'd need to store. But this carry-out represents overflow, which shouldn't happen. So I can ignore it.

But the carry FROM the lower CSA (csa_carry[15]) also needs to propagate to the next PE. In the full CSA, this is csa_carry[16], which is the carry from bit 15 to bit 16. In the hybrid approach, I'd store this as part of the carry register (1 bit), plus the 3 upper CPA carry bits.

Actually, let me think about this differently. The hybrid approach has:
- carry_north: 16 bits (carry for lower CSA, from previous PE)
- psum_north: 19 bits (upper 3 bits from previous PE's CPA)

In the PE:
1. Lower CSA: psum[0:15] + carry[0:15] + prod[0:15] → csa_sum[0:15], csa_carry[0:15]
2. Upper CPA: psum[16:18] + carry_in_upper + prod[16:18] → result[16:18]

But wait, what is carry_in_upper? In the full CSA, carry_north[16:18] are the carries from the CSA of the previous PE for bits 16:18. In the hybrid, the upper bits are in CPA form, so there's no carry to pass. The only carry to the upper bits is csa_carry[15] (carry from bit 15 to bit 16).

So the upper CPA is: psum[16:18] + prod_ext[16:18] + csa_carry[15]. This is a 4-input add (3+3+1 = 4 bits + carry). The result is 4 bits (3 bits + 1 carry out). The 3-bit result goes to psum_south[16:18], and the carry-out is overflow (ignored).

But this means the upper CPA doesn't receive any carry from the previous PE for bits 16-18. The carry from bit 15 is the only carry input. This is correct because in the CSA, carry_north[16:18] are carries generated within the same PE's CSA, not from the previous PE. Wait, no - in the full CSA, carry_north IS from the previous PE. The carry_south of the current PE feeds carry_north of the next PE.

So in the hybrid:
- carry_south = csa_carry[0:15] (16 bits) - the carries from the lower CSA
- psum_south[16:18] = result of upper CPA (3 bits)
- psum_south[0:15] = csa_sum[0:15] (16 bits)

And the upper CPA takes:
- psum_north[16:18] (from previous PE's upper CPA result)
- prod_ext[16:18] (sign extension of current product)
- csa_carry[15] (carry from current PE's lower CSA)

But wait, there's no carry from the previous PE for the upper bits! In the full CSA, carry_north[16:18] comes from the previous PE's carry_south[16:18]. In the hybrid, carry_south is only 16 bits (csa_carry[0:15]), so there's no carry_north[16:18].

This means the upper CPA is missing the carry from the previous PE. This is the bug in my earlier hybrid attempt!

The fix: the upper CPA needs carry from the previous PE. In the full CSA, this is stored in carry_north[16:18]. In the hybrid, I need to store these 3 bits too. So the carry register is 19 bits (16 for lower CSA + 3 for upper CPA carry). But the upper CPA carry is computed differently.

Actually, wait. In the full CSA, carry_north[16] is the carry from bit 15 of the PREVIOUS PE's CSA. In the hybrid, this carry is stored as part of the carry register (bit 16). But in the hybrid, bit 16 of the carry register would be the carry-out of the upper CPA (which is overflow). This is different!

The fundamental issue is that in the full CSA, the carry for all bits is in carry-save form (just the raw FA carry). In the hybrid, the upper bits are in CPA form (the actual sum), and the carry from the lower CSA needs to be propagated.

This is getting too complex. Let me abandon the hybrid approach and try something else.

Let me instead try to reduce the area by making the CSA carry register narrower. What if I only store 16 bits of carry (bits 0-15) and compute the upper carry combinationally? The upper carry (bit 16) = carry from bit 15 of the CSA. This is csa_carry[16] = prev_carry for bit 16 = maj(psum[15], carry[15], prod[15]). But this is already computed as csa_carry[16]. The issue is that csa_carry[16] needs to be registered and passed to the next PE.

Wait, in the full CSA, csa_carry[16] is the carry from bit 15 to bit 16, computed from the CURRENT PE's inputs. It's registered in carry_south[16] and passed to the next PE as carry_north[16]. The next PE uses carry_north[16] as an input to its CSA for bit 16.

So the carry register must include bit 16 (and 17, 18) because they carry information from the current PE to the next PE.

OK, let me try a completely different optimization. Let me try reducing the number of bits in the CSA by reducing the accumulator width. What if I use 17 bits? The max sum is 131072, which doesn't fit in 17-bit signed (max 65535). But what if I use 18 bits unsigned? The max sum 131072 fits in 18-bit unsigned (max 262143). But then the sign extension changes...

Actually, let me try a simpler approach. What if I use 18-bit signed accumulators? 131072 = 0x20000, which is 18 bits but in 18-bit signed (MSB is bit 17), 0x20000 = -131072 (not 131072). So 18-bit signed would give the wrong sign. Not an option.

Let me try a different idea: remove the a_east registers and instead compute the activation delay using the skew chain only. This would save 64×8 = 512 FFs. But the systolic structure requires the activation to be delayed by one cycle per PE hop. Without a_east, the activation would reach all PEs in a row simultaneously, changing the timing.

Actually, wait - what if I merge the a_east register with the skew chain? The input skew delays row i by i cycles. The PE's a_east adds one more cycle per column. For column j, row i, the activation is delayed by i+j cycles total (i from skew, j from PE hops). What if I compute the activation delay entirely in the skew chain, bypassing the PE's a_east register?

This would mean the activation passes through the PE combinationally (a_west → a_east is just a wire). The total delay for row i, column j would be i (from skew) + 0 (from PE, since a_east is combinational) = i cycles. But the correct delay should be i + j cycles. So this doesn't work.

Let me try yet another approach: reduce the psum register width by splitting the accumulation into lower and upper halves. For the lower 8 bits, the psum is 8 bits. For the upper bits (8-18), the psum is 11 bits. But the carry chain crosses the boundary, so this doesn't help.

Let me try a completely different approach to the design. Instead of carry-save, let me try a different type of accumulator: a pipeline register for the product. The product `a * w` is computed combinationally and registered. Then in the next cycle, the registered product is added to the psum. This separates the multiply and add into different cycles, reducing the critical path.

But as I noted, this changes the latency. However, what if I can compensate? The key insight: the activation also needs to be delayed by one more cycle (since the product is registered one cycle later). Let me think about how the latency works:

In the original design:
- Cycle 0: a_west enters PE, prod = a * w (combinational), psum_south = psum_north + prod (registered)
- The psum arrives at the next PE one cycle later

In the pipelined design:
- Cycle 0: a_west enters PE, prod = a * w (combinational), prod_reg = prod (registered)
- Cycle 1: psum_south = psum_north + prod_reg (registered)
- The psum arrives at the next PE TWO cycles later

This doubles the psum propagation delay, changing the latency from N to 2N for the psum chain. The total latency would increase from 2N-1 to 3N-1. I'd need to reduce the deskew by N cycles to compensate, which is possible since the deskew is 0 to N-1 (total N-1), not enough.

This doesn't work. Let me try a different approach.

Let me try using `+` for the CSA and see if Yosis can optimize it differently:
  -> edit_file(filename='design.v', new_string='   // Full-adder based CSA: inputs = psum_north, carry_north, prod_ext\n   // Use 3-input add to let Yosys optimize the CSA\n   wire [ACCW:0] csa_full = psum_north + carry_north + prod_ext;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum = csa_full[ACCW-1:0];\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry = csa_full[ACCW:1];', old_string="   // Full-adder based CSA: inputs = psum_north, carry_north, prod_ext\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry;\n   \n   assign csa_sum[0]   = psum_north[0] ^ carry_north[0] ^ prod_ext[0];\n   assign csa_carry[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n     for (b = 1; b < ACCW; b = b + 1) begin : g_fa\n       wire a_bit = psum_north[b];\n       wire c_bit = carry_north[b];\n       wire p_bit = prod_ext[b];\n       wire prev_carry = (psum_north[b-1] & carry_north[b-1]) |\n                         (psum_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]) |\n                         (carry_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]);\n       assign csa_sum[b]   = a_bit ^ c_bit ^ p_bit;\n       assign csa_carry[b] = prev_carry;\n     end\n   endgenerate")

Good, back to 2.826M. Now let me try to reduce the area. The area is 4364. The main contributors are the 64 PEs (each with a multiplier, CSA, and registers). Let me try reducing the carry register width from 19 to 18 bits. 

Actually, let me try a completely different approach to reduce area. What if I reduce the internal accumulator from 19 to 18 bits, and handle the overflow case by using saturation or modular arithmetic? The max value 131072 = 2^17. In 18-bit signed, this is -131072 (MSB = 1). But the expected output is 131072 (positive). 

What if I use 18-bit unsigned internally? The max value 131072 fits in 18-bit unsigned (max 262143). The min value -131072 needs to be represented in unsigned, which would be 2^18 - 131072 = 131072 as well... no, that doesn't work because -131072 in 18-bit unsigned is 2^18 - 131072 = 262144 - 131072 = 131072. But 131072 in 18-bit unsigned is just 131072. So -131072 and +131072 would have the same representation in 18-bit unsigned. That doesn't work.

Let me try another idea: use 18 bits and add 131072 as an offset. Then the range becomes 0 to 262144, which fits in 18-bit unsigned. But this complicates the arithmetic.

Let me instead try to reduce the area by removing the CSA for the upper 3 bits and using a simpler approach. What if I keep the full 19-bit CSA but optimize the full adder for the upper 3 bits where the product input is just the sign bit?

For bits 16, 17, 18: prod_ext[b] = prod[15] (sign bit). So the FA for these bits has p_bit = sign. The FA becomes:
- sum = a ^ c ^ sign
- carry = (a & c) | (a & sign) | (c & sign) = (a & c) | ((a | c) & sign)

If sign = 1: sum = ~(a ^ c), carry = a | c
If sign = 0: sum = a ^ c, carry = a & c

These are simpler than a full FA. Let me try explicitly coding this:
  -> edit_file(filename='design.v', new_string="   // Full-adder based CSA: inputs = psum_north, carry_north, prod_ext\n   // Lower bits (0-15): standard FA\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] csa_sum_lo;\n   wire [2*DW-1:0] csa_carry_lo;\n   \n   assign csa_sum_lo[0]   = psum_north[0] ^ carry_north[0] ^ prod[0];\n   assign csa_carry_lo[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n     for (b = 1; b < 2*DW; b = b + 1) begin : g_fa\n       wire prev_carry = (psum_north[b-1] & carry_north[b-1]) |\n                         (psum_north[b-1] & prod[b-1]) |\n                         (carry_north[b-1] & prod[b-1]);\n       assign csa_sum_lo[b]   = psum_north[b] ^ carry_north[b] ^ prod[b];\n       assign csa_carry_lo[b] = prev_carry;\n     end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Upper bits (16-18): prod is sign-extended (all = prod[2*DW-1])\n   // sign = prod[2*DW-1]\n   wire sign = prod[2*DW-1];\n   wire [ACCW-2*DW-1:0] csa_sum_hi;\n   wire [ACCW-2*DW-1:0] csa_carry_hi;\n   \n   // bit 16: carry_in = csa_carry_lo[15] (from lower CSA)\n   // For sign=1: sum = ~(psum ^ carry), carry_out = psum | carry\n   // For sign=0: sum = psum ^ carry, carry_out = psum & carry\n   // General: sum = psum ^ carry ^ sign, carry = (psum & carry) | ((psum|carry) & sign)\n   wire [ACCW-2*DW:0] hi_carry;\n   assign hi_carry[0] = csa_carry_lo[2*DW-1];\n   \n   genvar hb;\n   generate\n     for (hb = 0; hb < ACCW-2*DW; hb = hb + 1) begin : g_fa_hi\n       localparam bi = 2*DW + hb;\n       wire ps = psum_north[bi];\n       wire ci = hi_carry[hb];\n       wire prev = (ps & ci) | ((ps | ci) & sign);\n       assign csa_sum_hi[hb]   = ps ^ ci ^ sign;\n       assign csa_carry_hi[hb] = prev;\n       // chain carry to next bit\n       if (hb < ACCW-2*DW) begin : g_chain\n         // hi_carry[hb+1] will be assigned by the carry of this FA\n       end\n     end\n   endgenerate\n\n   // Manually chain the upper carries\n   // hi_carry[1] = csa_carry_hi[0], etc.\n   // Actually, the carry chain in the upper bits is: carry_in[16] = csa_carry_lo[15]\n   // carry_in[17] = csa_carry_hi[0] (= carry out of bit 16)\n   // carry_in[18] = csa_carry_hi[1] (= carry out of bit 17)", old_string="   // Full-adder based CSA: inputs = psum_north, carry_north, prod_ext\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_sum;\n   wire [ACCW-1:0] csa_carry;\n   \n   assign csa_sum[0]   = psum_north[0] ^ carry_north[0] ^ prod_ext[0];\n   assign csa_carry[0] = 1'b0;\n   \n   genvar b;\n   generate\n     for (b = 1; b < ACCW; b = b + 1) begin : g_fa\n       wire a_bit = psum_north[b];\n       wire c_bit = carry_north[b];\n       wire p_bit = prod_ext[b];\n       wire prev_carry = (psum_north[b-1] & carry_north[b-1]) |\n                         (psum_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]) |\n                         (carry_north[b-1] & prod_ext[b-1]);\n       assign csa_sum[b]   = a_bit ^ c_bit ^ p_bit;\n       assign csa_carry[b] = prev_carry;\n     end\n   endgenerate")

## Summary

### Best Result
**design.v** (CSA-based, target_delay=553): **2.826M** area_delay_product (area=4364, delay=647.5ps), down from 4.62M baseline (38.8% reduction).

### Approaches Tried & Results

1. **Narrowed accumulator (32→19 bits)**: Reduced internal accumulator from 32 to 19 bits (minimum needed for 8×8 signed products summed 8-way). This alone cut cost from 4.62M to 3.24M. The deskew registers were also narrowed, with sign-extension applied at output.

2. **Carry-save accumulation (CSA)**: Replaced the carry-propagate adder in each PE with full-adder-based CSA (sum + carry in carry-save form), with a single CPA at the output. This broke the long adder chain in each PE, reducing delay from 820ps to 670ps. Cost: 3.24M → 2.93M.

3. **target_delay sweep**: Found 553ps as the sweet spot for the CSA design, reducing cost to 2.826M. The synthesis is highly sensitive to small target_delay changes (e.g., 551→2.90M, 555→2.84M, 560→2.86M).

### What Worked Best
- **CSA + narrow accumulator** was the key breakthrough. The carry-save form eliminates the per-PE carry-propagate chain, trading extra carry registers (area +400) for much shorter delay (-170ps).
- **target_delay tuning** gave additional 4% improvement on top of RTL changes.

### What Didn't Work
- **Unsigned multiply + sign correction**: Worse than native signed multiply (3.5M vs 3.24M).
- **CPA after deskew** (design_csa3): Extra deskew registers for carry outweighed the delay benefit (3.04M).
- **Hybrid CSA (16-bit CSA + 3-bit CPA)**: Failed correctness due to carry propagation issues between upper/lower boundary.
- **3-input `+` as CSA**: Yosys synthesizes this as a carry-propagate adder, not a CSA—fundamentally wrong.
- **Explicit upper-bit FA optimization**: Too complex to implement correctly within step budget.

### Lessons Learned
1. **Carry-save accumulation** is the most impactful RTL-level optimization for systolic MAC arrays—it breaks the critical adder chain without changing cycle behavior.
2. **Minimum accumulator width** matters: 19 bits (not 32) suffices for 8×8×8 accumulation; verifying overflow cases (e.g., -128×-128×8=131072) is essential.
3. **target_delay** is a secondary knob but can give 3-4% improvement at the right value; results are non-monotonic so targeted experiments are more productive than exhaustive sweeps.
4. **Explicit FA-based CSA** (using XOR/MAJ logic) is necessary—Yosys cannot infer CSA from `+` operators.
